And wherefore will I do it?<br>I tore them from their bonds, and cried aloud,<br>‘O that these hands could so redeem my son,<br>As they have given these hairs their liberty!’<br>But now I envy at their liberty,<br>And will again commit them to their bonds,<br>Because my poor [[Child\|child]] is a prisoner. 75<br>She binds up her hair<br>And Father Cardinal, I have heard you say<br>That we shall see and know our friends in heaven.<br>If that be true, I shall see my boy again;<br>For since the birth of [[Cain]], the first male [[Child\|child]],<br>To him that did but yesterday suspire, 80<br>There was not such a gracious creature born.<br>But now will canker-sorrow eat my bud,<br>And chase the native [[Beauty\|beauty]] from his cheek;<br>And he will look as hollow as a [[Ghost\|ghost]],<br>As dim and meagre as an ague’s fit,<br>And so he’ll die; and rising so again,<br>When I shall meet him in the [[Court\|court]] of heaven,<br>I shall not know him; therefore never, never<br>Must I behold my pretty [[Arthur]] more. |
| PANDOLF | You hold too heinous a respect of grief. |
| [[Constance\|CONSTANCE]] | He talks to me that never had a son. |
| [[King\|KING]] PHILIP | You are as fond of grief as of your [[Child\|child]]. |
| [[Constance\|CONSTANCE]] | Grief fills the room up of my absent [[Child\|child]],<br>Lies in his bed, walks up and down with me,<br>Puts on his pretty looks, repeats his words,<br>Remembers me of all his gracious parts,<br>Stuffs out his vacant garments with his form;<br>Then have I reason to be fond of grief.<br>Fare you [[Well\|well]]. Had you such a loss as I,<br>I could give better comfort than you do. 100 |
| *(didascalia)* | She unbinds her hair |
| [[Constance\|CONSTANCE]] | I will not keep this form upon my head<br>When there is such disorder in my wit.<br>O Lord, my boy, my [[Arthur]], my fair son,<br>My life, my joy, my food, my all the world, 104<br>My widow-comfort, and my sorrow’s cure! Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | I [[Fear\|fear]] some outrage, and I’ll follow her. Exit attended |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | There’s nothing in this world can make me joy.<br>Life is as tedious as a twice-told tale,<br>Vexing the dull ear of a drowsy man;<br>And bitter shame hath spoiled the sweet world’s taste,<br>That it yields naught but shame and bitterness. 111 |
| PANDOLF | Before the curing of a strong disease,<br>Even in the instant of repair and health,<br>The fit is strongest. Evils that take leave,<br>On their departure most of all show evil. 115<br>What have you lost by losing of this day? |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | All days of glory, joy, and happiness. |
| PANDOLF | If you had won it, certainly you had.<br>No, no; when Fortune means to men most good,<br>She looks upon them with a threat‘ning eye.<br>’Tis strange to think how much [[King John]] hath lost<br>In this which he accounts so clearly won.<br>Are not you grieved that [[Arthur]] is his prisoner? |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | As heartily as he is glad he hath him. |
| PANDOLF | Your mind is all as youthful as your [[Blood\|blood]]. 125<br>Now hear me speak with a prophetic spirit,<br>For even the breath of what I mean to speak<br>Shall blow each [[Dust\|dust]], each straw, each little rub,<br>Out of the path which shall directly lead<br>Thy foot to England’s throne. And therefore mark.<br>John hath seized [[Arthur]], and it cannot be<br>That whiles warm life plays in that infant’s veins<br>The misplaced John should entertain an hour,<br>One minute, nay, one quiet breath of rest.<br>A sceptre snatched with an unruly hand<br>Must be as boisterously maintained as gained;<br>And he that stands upon a slipp’ry place<br>Makes nice of no vile hold to stay him up.<br>That John may stand, then [[Arthur]] needs must fall;<br>So be it, for it cannot be but so. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | But what shall I gain by young [[Arthur]]’s fall? |
| PANDOLF | You, in the right of Lady Blanche your wife,<br>May then make all the claim that [[Arthur]] did. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | And lose it, life and all, as [[Arthur]] did. |
| PANDOLF | How green you are, and fresh in this old world 145<br>John lays you plots; the times conspire with you;<br>For he that steeps his safety in true [[Blood\|blood]]<br>Shall find but bloody safety and untrue.<br>This act, so vilely born, shall cool the hearts<br>Of all his people, and freeze up their zeal,<br>That none so small advantage shall step forth<br>To check his reign but they will cherish it;<br>No natural exhalation in the sky,<br>No scope of [[Nature\|nature]], no distempered day,<br>No common [[Wind\|wind]], no customèd event, 155<br>But they will pluck away his natural cause,<br>And call them meteors, prodigies, and signs,<br>Abortives, presages, and tongues of heaven<br>Plainly denouncing vengeance upon John. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | Maybe he will not touch young [[Arthur]]’s life,<br>But hold himself safe in his prisonment. |
| PANDOLF | O sir, when he shall hear of your approach,<br>If that young [[Arthur]] be not gone already,<br>Even at that news he dies; and then the hearts<br>Of all his people shall revolt from him,<br>And kiss the lips of unacquainted change,<br>And pick strong matter of revolt and wrath<br>Out of the bloody fingers’ ends of John.<br>Methinks I see this hurly all on foot,<br>And O, what better matter breeds for you 170<br>Than I have named! The Bastard Falconbridge<br>Is now in England, ransacking the Church,<br>Offending [[Charity\|charity]]. If but a dozen French<br>Were there in arms, they would be as a call<br>To train ten thousand English to their side, 175<br>Or as a little [[Snow\|snow]] tumbled about<br>Anon becomes a mountain. O noble [[Dauphin]],<br>Go with me to the [[King]]. ’Tis wonderful<br>What may be wrought out of their discontent<br>Now that their souls are top-full of offence. 180<br>For England, go! I will whet on the [[King]]. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | Strong reasons make strange actions. Let us go.<br>If you say ay, the [[King]] will not say no. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Hubert]], and Executioners with a rope and irons |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Heat me these irons hot, and look thou stand |
| *(didascalia)* | Within the arras. When I strike my foot |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Upon the bosom of the ground, rush forth<br>And bind the boy which you shall find with me<br>Fast to the chair. Be heedful. Hence, and watch! 5 |
| EXECUTIONER | I [[Hope\|hope]] your warrant will bear out the deed. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Uncleanly scruples: [[Fear\|fear]] not you. Look to’t! |
| *(didascalia)* | The Executioners withdraw behind the arras |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Young lad, come forth, I have to say with you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Arthur]] Duke of Brittaine |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | Good morrow, [[Hubert]].<br>Good morrow, little Prince. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | As little prince, having so great a title 10<br>To be more prince, as may be. You are sad. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Indeed I have been merrier.<br>ARTHURMercy on me!<br>Methinks nobody should be sad but I.<br>Yet I remember, when I was in France,<br>Young gentlemen would be as sad as [[Night\|night]] 15<br>Only for wantonness. By my christendom,<br>So I were out of [[Prison\|prison]] and kept sheep,<br>I should be as merry as the day is long;<br>And so I would be here, but that I [[Doubt\|doubt]]<br>My uncle practises more harm to me. 20<br>He is afraid of me, and I of him.<br>Is it my fault that I was Geoffrey’s son?<br>No, indeed is’t not, and I would to God<br>I were your son, so you would [[Love\|love]] me, [[Hubert]]. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | (aside)<br>If I talk to him, with his [[Innocent\|innocent]] prate 25<br>He will awake my [[Mercy\|mercy]], which lies dead;<br>Therefore I will be sudden, and dispatch. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | Are you sick, [[Hubert]]? You look pale today.<br>In sooth, I would you were a little sick,<br>That I might sit all [[Night\|night]] and watch with you. 30<br>I warrant I [[Love\|love]] you more than you do me.<br>[[Hubert\|HUBERT]](aside)<br>His words do take possession of my bosom.<br>He shows [[Arthur]] a paper<br>Read here, young [[Arthur]]. (Aside) How now: foolish<br>rheum,<br>Turning dispiteous torture out of door?<br>I must be brief, lest resolution drop<br>Out at mine eyes in tender womanish [[Tears\|tears]].<br>(To [[Arthur]]) Can you not read it? Is it not fair writ? |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | Too fairly, [[Hubert]], for so foul effect.<br>Must you with hot irons burn out both mine eyes? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Young boy, I must. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | And will you?<br>HUBERTAnd I will. 40 |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | Have you the heart? When your head did but ache<br>I knit my handkerchief about your brows,<br>The best I had—a princess wrought it me,<br>And I did never ask it you again—<br>And with my hand at midnight held your head,<br>And like the watchful minutes to the hour<br>Still and anon cheered up the heavy [[Time\|time]],<br>Saying ‘What lack you?’ and ‘Where lies your grief?’<br>Or ‘What good [[Love\|love]] may I perform for you?’<br>Many a poor man’s son would have lain still<br>And ne’er have spoke a loving word to you,<br>But you at your sick service had a prince.<br>Nay, you may think my [[Love\|love]] was crafty [[Love\|love]],<br>And call it cunning. Do, an if you will.<br>If heaven be pleased that you must use me ill, 55<br>Why then you must. Will you put out mine eyes,<br>These eyes that never did, nor never shall,<br>So much as frown on you? |
| HUBERTI | have sworn to do it,<br>And with hot irons must I burn them out. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | Ah, none but in this iron age would do it. 60<br>The iron of itself, though heat red hot,<br>Approaching near these eyes would drink my [[Tears\|tears]],<br>And quench his fiery indignation<br>Even in the matter of mine [[innocence]];<br>Nay, after that, consume away in rust,<br>But for containing [[Fire\|fire]] to harm mine eye.<br>Are you more stubborn-hard than hammered iron?<br>An if an angel should have come to me<br>And told me [[Hubert]] should put out mine eyes,<br>I would not have believed him; no tongue but<br>[[Hubert]]’s.<br>[[Hubert]] stamps his foot |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Come forth!<br>The Executioners come forth<br>Do as I bid you do. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | O, save me, [[Hubert]], save me! My eyes are out<br>Even with the fierce looks of these bloody men. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | (to the Executioners)<br>Give me the iron, I say, and bind him here.<br>He takes the iron |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | Alas, what need you be so boisterous-rough?<br>I will not struggle; I will stand stone-still.<br>For God’s sake, [[Hubert]], let me not be bound.<br>Nay, hear me, [[Hubert]] Drive these men away,<br>And I will sit as quiet as a [[Lamb\|lamb]];<br>I will not stir, nor wince, nor speak a word, 80<br>Nor look upon the iron angerly.<br>Thrust but these men away, and I’ll forgive you,<br>Whatever torment you do put me to. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | (to the Executioners)<br>Go stand within. Let me [[Alone\|alone]] with him. |
| EXECUTIONER | I am best pleased to be from such a deed. 85 |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Executioners |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | Alas, I then have chid away my friend!<br>He hath a stern look, but a gentle heart.<br>Let him come back, that his compassion may<br>Give life to yours. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Come, boy, prepare yourself. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | Is there no remedy? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | None but to lose your eyes. 90 |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | O God, that there were but a mote in yours,<br>A grain, a [[Dust\|dust]], a gnat, a wandering hair,<br>Any annoyance in that precious sense,<br>Then, feeling what small things are boisterous there,<br>Your vile intent must needs seem horrible. 95 |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Is this your promise? Go to, hold your tongue! |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | [[Hubert]], the utterance of a brace of tongues<br>Must needs want pleading for a pair of eyes.<br>Let me not hold my tongue, let me not, [[Hubert]];<br>Or, [[Hubert]], if you will, cut out my tongue, 100<br>So I may keep mine eyes. O, spare mine eyes,<br>Though to no use but still to look on you.<br>Lo, by my troth, the instrument is cold<br>And would not harm me. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | I can heat it, boy. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | No, in good sooth: the [[Fire\|fire]] is dead with grief, 105<br>Being create for comfort, to be used<br>In undeserved extremes. See else yourself.<br>There is no malice in this burning coal;<br>The breath of heaven hath blown his spirit out,<br>And strewed repentant ashes on his head. II0 |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | But with my breath I can revive it, boy. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | An if you do, you will but make it blush<br>And glow with shame of your proceedings, [[Hubert]].<br>Nay, it perchance will sparkle in your eyes,<br>And like a dog that is compelled to fight,<br>Snatch at his master that doth tarre him on.<br>All things that you should use to do me wrong<br>Deny their office; only you do lack<br>That [[Mercy\|mercy]] which fierce [[Fire\|fire]] and iron extends,<br>Creatures of note for [[Mercy\|mercy]]-lacking uses. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | [[Well]], see to live. I will not touch thine eye<br>For all the treasure that thine uncle owes.<br>Yet am I sworn, and I did purpose, boy,<br>With this same very iron to burn them out. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | O, now you look like [[Hubert]]. All this while<br>You were disguised. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Peace, no more. Adieu.<br>Your uncle must not know but you are dead.<br>I’ll fill these dogged spies with false reports;<br>And, pretty [[Child\|child]], [[Sleep\|sleep]] doubtless and secure<br>That [[Hubert]], for the wealth of all the world,<br>Will not offend thee. |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | O God! I thank you, [[Hubert]]. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | [[Silence]], no more. Go closely in with me.<br>Much danger do I undergo for thee. Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | 4.2 flourish. Enter [[King John]], the Earls of Pembroke and Salisbury, and other lords. [[King John]] ascends the throne |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Here once again we sit, once again crowned,<br>And looked upon, I [[Hope\|hope]], with cheerful eyes. |
| PEMBROKE | This ‘once again’, but that your highness pleased,<br>Was once superfluous. You were crowned before,<br>And that high royalty was ne‘er plucked off,<br>The faiths of men ne’er stained with revolt;<br>Fresh expectation troubled not the land<br>With any longed-for change or better state. |
| SALISBURY | Therefore to be possessed with [[Double\|double]] pomp,<br>To guard a title that was rich before, 10<br>To gild refined gold, to paint the lily,<br>To throw a perfume on the violet,<br>To smooth the ice, or add another hue<br>Unto the rainbow, or with taper-light<br>To seek the beauteous eye of heaven to garnish,<br>Is wasteful and ridiculous excess. |
| PEMBROKE | But that your royal pleasure must be done,<br>This act is as an ancient tale new-told,<br>And in the last repeating troublesome,<br>Being urged at a [[Time\|time]] unseasonable. |
| SALISBURY | In this the antique and [[Well\|well]]-noted face<br>Of plain old form is much disfigured,<br>And like a shifted [[Wind\|wind]] unto a sail,<br>It makes the course of thoughts to fetch about,<br>Startles and frights consideration,<br>Makes sound opinion sick, and [[Truth\|truth]] suspected<br>For putting on so new a fashioned robe. |
| PEMBROKE | When workmen strive to do better than [[Well\|well]],<br>They do confound their skill in covetousness;<br>And oftentimes excusing of a fault<br>Doth make the fault the worser by th’excuse;<br>As patches set upon a little breach<br>Discredit more in hiding of the fault<br>Than did the fault before it was so patched. |
| SALISBURY | To this effect: before you were new-crowned<br>We breathed our counsel, but it pleased your<br>highness<br>To overbear it; and we are all [[Well\|well]] pleased,<br>Since all and every part of what we would<br>Doth make a stand at what your highness will. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Some reasons of this [[Double\|double]] coronation<br>I have possessed you with, and think them strong.<br>And more, more strong, when lesser is my [[Fear\|fear]]<br>I shall endue you with. Meantime but ask<br>What you would have reformed that is not [[Well\|well]],<br>And [[Well\|well]] shall you perceive how willingly<br>I will both hear and grant you your requests. |
| PEMBROKE | Then I, as one that am the tongue of these<br>To sound the purposes of all their hearts,<br>Both for myself and them, but chief of all<br>Your safety, for the which myself and them<br>Bend their best studies, heartily request<br>Th’enfranchisement of [[Arthur]], whose restraint<br>Doth move the murmuring lips of discontent<br>To break into this dangerous argument:<br>If what in rest you have, in right you hold, 55<br>Why then your fears—which, as they say, attend<br>The steps of wrong—should move you to mew up<br>Your tender kinsman, and to choke his days<br>With barbarous ignorance, and deny his youth<br>The rich advantage of good exercise?<br>That the [[Time\|time]]’s enemies may not have this<br>To grace occasions, let it be our suit<br>That you have bid us ask, his liberty;<br>Which for our goods we do no further ask<br>Than whereupon our weal, on you depending, 65<br>Counts it your weal he have his liberty. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Hubert]] |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Let it be so. I do commit his youth<br>To your direction.—[[Hubert]], what news with you?<br>He takes [[Hubert]] aside |
| PEMBROKE | This is the man should do the bloody deed:<br>He showed his warrant to a friend of mine. 70<br>The image of a wicked heinous fault<br>Lives in his eye; that close aspect of his<br>Does show the mood of a much troubled breast;<br>And I do fearfully believe ’tis done<br>What we so feared he had a charge to do. 75 |
| SALISBURY | The colour of the [[King]] doth come and go<br>Between his purpose and his [[Conscience\|conscience]],<br>Like heralds ’twixt two dreadful battles set.<br>His passion is so ripe it needs must break. |
| PEMBROKE | And when it breaks, I [[Fear\|fear]] will issue thence 80<br>The foul corruption of a sweet [[Child\|child]]’s [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | (coming forward)<br>We cannot hold [[Mortality\|mortality]]’s strong hand.<br>Good lords, although my will to give is living,<br>The suit which you demand is gone and dead.<br>He tells us [[Arthur]] is deceased tonight. |
| SALISBURY | Indeed we feared his sickness was past cure. |
| PEMBROKE | Indeed we heard how near his [[Death\|death]] he was,<br>Before the [[Child\|child]] himself felt he was sick.<br>This must be answered, either here or hence. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Why do you bend such solemn brows on me? 90<br>Think you I bear the shears of destiny?<br>Have I commandment on the pulse of life? |
| SALISBURY | It is apparent foul play, and ’tis shame<br>That [[Greatness\|greatness]] should so grossly offer it.<br>So thrive it in your game; and so, farewell. |
| PEMBROKE | Stay yet, Lord Salisbury; I’ll go with thee,<br>And find th’inheritance of this poor [[Child\|child]],<br>His little kingdom of a forced [[Grave\|grave]].<br>That [[Blood\|blood]] which owed the breadth of all this isle<br>Three foot of it doth hold. Bad world the while. 100<br>This must not be thus borne. This will break out<br>To all our sorrows; and ere long, I [[Doubt\|doubt]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Pembroke, Salisbury, and other lords |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | They burn in indignation. I repent.<br>There is no sure foundation set on [[Blood\|blood]],<br>No certain life achieved by others’ [[Death\|death]]. 105 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | A fearful eye thou hast. Where is that [[Blood\|blood]]<br>That I have seen inhabit in those cheeks?<br>So foul a sky clears not without a [[Storm\|storm]];<br>Pour down thy weather: how goes all in France? |
| MESSENGER | From France to England. Never such a power<br>For any foreign preparation<br>Was levied in the [[Body\|body]] of a land.<br>The copy of your [[Speed\|speed]] is learned by them,<br>For when you should be told they do prepare,<br>The tidings comes that they are all arrived. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | O, where hath our intelligence been drunk?<br>Where hath it slept? Where is my mother’s ear,<br>That such an army could be drawn in France,<br>And she not hear of it? |
| MESSENGER | My liege, her ear<br>Is stopped with [[Dust\|dust]]. The first of April died<br>Your noble mother. And as I hear, my lord,<br>The Lady [[Constance]] in a frenzy died<br>Three days before; but this from rumour’s tongue<br>I idly heard; if true or false I know not. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Withhold thy [[Speed\|speed]], dreadful Occasion;<br>O, make a league with me till I have pleased<br>My discontented peers. What, Mother dead?<br>How wildly then walks my estate in France!—<br>Under whose conduct came those powers of France<br>That thou for [[Truth\|truth]] giv’st out are landed here? |
| MESSENGER | Under the [[Dauphin]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Bastard and Peter of Pomfret |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Thou hast made me giddy<br>With these ill tidings. (To the Bastard) Now, what says<br>the world<br>To your proceedings? Do not seek to stuff<br>My head with more ill news, for it is full. |
| BASTARD | But if you,be afeard to hear the worst, 135<br>Then let the worst, unheard, fall on your head. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Bear with me, cousin, for I was amazed<br>Under the tide; but now I breathe again<br>Aloft the flood, and can give audience<br>To any tongue, speak it of what it will. |
| BASTARD | How I have sped among the clergymen<br>The sums I have collected shall express.<br>But as I travelled hither through the land,<br>I find the people strangely fantasied,<br>Possessed with rumours, full of idle [[Dreams\|dreams]],<br>Not knowing what they [[Fear\|fear]], but full of [[Fear\|fear]].<br>And here’s a [[Prophet\|prophet]] that I brought with me<br>From forth the streets of Pomfret, whom I found<br>With many hundreds treading on his heels;<br>To whom he sung, in rude, harsh-sounding rhymes,<br>That ere the next Ascension Day at [[Noon\|noon]] 151<br>Your highness should deliver up your [[Crown\|crown]]. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Thou idle dreamer, wherefore didst thou so? |
| PETER OF POMFRET | Foreknowing that the [[Truth\|truth]] will fall out so. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | [[Hubert]], away with him! Imprison him,155<br>And on that day, at [[Noon\|noon]], whereon he says<br>I shall yield up my [[Crown\|crown]], let him be hanged.<br>Deliver him to safety, and return,<br>For I must use thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Hubert]] and Peter of Pomfret |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | O my gentle cousin,<br>Hear’st thou the news abroad, who are arrived? 160 |
| BASTARD | The French, my lord: men’s mouths are full of it.<br>Besides, I met Lord Bigot and Lord Salisbury<br>With eyes as red as new-enkindled [[Fire\|fire]],<br>And others more, going to seek the [[Grave\|grave]]<br>Of [[Arthur]], whom they say is killed tonight<br>On your suggestion. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Gentle kinsman, go<br>And thrust thyself into their companies.<br>I have away to win their loves again.<br>Bring them before me. |
| BASTARD | I will seek them out. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Nay, but make haste, the better foot before.<br>O, let me have no subject enemies<br>When adverse foreigners affright my towns<br>With dreadful pomp of stout invasion!<br>Be Mercury, set feathers to thy heels,<br>And fly like thought from them to me again. 175 |
| BASTARD | The spirit of the [[Time\|time]] shall teach me [[Speed\|speed]]. Exit |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Spoke like a sprightful noble gentleman!—<br>Go after him, for he perhaps shall need<br>Some messenger betwixt me and the peers,<br>And be thou he. 180 |
| MESSENGER | With all my heart, my liege. Exit |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | My mother dead! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Hubert]] |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | My lord, they say five moons were seen tonight,<br>Four fixed, and the fifth did whirl about<br>The other four in wondrous motion. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Five moons? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Old men and beldams in the streets<br>Do prophesy upon it dangerously.<br>Young [[Arthur]]’s [[Death\|death]] is common in their mouths,<br>And when they talk of him they shake their heads,<br>And whisper one another in the ear; 190<br>And he that speaks doth grip the hearer’s wrist,<br>Whilst he that hears makes fearful action,<br>With wrinkled brows, with nods, with [[rolling]] eyes.<br>I saw a [[smith]] stand with his hammer, thus,<br>The whilst his iron did on the anvil cool,<br>With open mouth swallowing a tailor’s news,<br>Who, with his shears and measure in his hand,<br>Standing on slippers which his nimble haste<br>Had falsely thrust upon contrary feet,<br>Told of a many thousand warlike French 200<br>That were embattailèd and ranked in [[Kent]].<br>Another lean unwashed artificer<br>Cuts off his tale, and talks of [[Arthur]]’s [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Why seek’st thou to possess me with these fears?<br>Why urgest thou so oft young [[Arthur]]’s [[Death\|death]]?<br>Thy hand hath murdered him. I had a mighty cause<br>To wish him dead, but thou hadst none to kill him. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | No had, my lord? Why, did you not provoke me? |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | It is the curse of kings to be attended<br>By slaves that take their humours for a warrant<br>To break within the bloody [[house]] of life,<br>And on the winking of authority<br>To understand a law, to know the meaning<br>Of dangerous majesty, when perchance it frowns<br>More upon [[Humour\|humour]] than advised respect. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Here is your hand and seal for what I did.<br>He shows a paper |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | O, when the last account ’twixt heaven and earth<br>Is to be made, then shall this hand and seal<br>Witness against us to damnation!<br>How oft the sight of means to do ill deeds 220<br>Make deeds ill done! Hadst not thou been by,<br>A fellow by the hand of [[Nature\|nature]] marked,<br>Quoted, and signed to do a deed of shame,<br>This murder had not come into my mind.<br>But taking note of thy abhorred aspect,<br>Finding thee fit for bloody villainy,<br>Apt, liable to be employed in danger,<br>I faintly broke with thee of [[Arthur]]’s [[Death\|death]];<br>And thou, to be endeared to a [[King\|king]],<br>Made it no [[Conscience\|conscience]] to destroy a prince. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | My lord— |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Hadst thou but shook thy head or made a pause<br>When I spake darkly what I purposed,<br>Or turned an eye of [[Doubt\|doubt]] upon my face,<br>As bid me tell my tale in express words,<br>Deep shame had struck me dumb, made me break off,<br>And those thy fears might have wrought fears in me.<br>But thou didst understand me by my signs,<br>And didst in signs again parley with sin;<br>Yea, without stop, didst let thy heart consent,<br>And consequently thy rude hand to act<br>The deed which both our tongues held vile to name.<br>Out of my sight, and never see me more!<br>My nobles leave me, and my state is braved,<br>Even at my gates, with ranks of foreign powers;<br>Nay, in the [[Body\|body]] of this fleshly land,<br>This kingdom, this confine of [[Blood\|blood]] and breath,<br>Hostility and civil tumult reigns<br>Between my [[Conscience\|conscience]] and my cousin’s [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Arm you against your other enemies;<br>I’ll make a peace between your [[Soul\|soul]] and you.<br>Young [[Arthur]] is alive. This hand of mine<br>Is yet a maiden and an [[Innocent\|innocent]] hand,<br>Not painted with the crimson spots of [[Blood\|blood]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Within this bosom never entered yet |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | The dreadful motion of a murderous thought;<br>And you have slandered [[Nature\|nature]] in my form,<br>Which, howsoever rude exteriorly,<br>Is yet the cover of a fairer mind<br>Than to be butcher of an [[Innocent\|innocent]] [[Child\|child]]. 260 |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Doth [[Arthur]] live? O, haste thee to the peers;<br>Throw this report on their incensed rage,<br>And make them tame to their obedience.<br>Forgive the comment that my passion made<br>Upon thy feature, for my rage was blind, 265<br>And foul imaginary eyes of [[Blood\|blood]]<br>Presented thee more hideous than thou art.<br>O,answer not, but to my closet bring<br>The angry lords with all expedient haste.<br>I conjure thee but slowly; run more fast. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 4, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Arthur]] Duke of Brittaine on the walls, disguised as a [[Ship\|ship]]-boy |
| [[Arthur\|ARTHUR]] | The wall is high, and yet will I leap down.<br>Good ground, be pitiful, and hurt me not.<br>There’s few or none do know me; if they did,<br>This [[Ship\|ship]]-boy’s semblance hath disguised me quite.<br>I am afraid, and yet I’ll venture it.<br>If I get down and do not break my limbs,<br>I’ll find a thousand shifts to get away.<br>As good to die and go, as die and stay.<br>He leaps down<br>O me! My uncle’s spirit is in these stones.<br>Heaven take my [[Soul\|soul]], and England keep my bones! I0<br>He dies Enter the Earls of Pembroke and Salisbury, and Lord Bigot |
| SALISBURY | Lords, I will meet him at Saint Edmundsbury.<br>It is our safety, and we must embrace<br>This gentle offer of the perilous [[Time\|time]]. |
| PEMBROKE | Who brought that letter from the Cardinal? |
| SALISBURY | The Count Melun, a noble lord of France,<br>Who’s private with me of the [[Dauphin]]’s [[Love\|love]];<br>’Tis much more general than these lines import. |
| BIGOT | Tomorrow morning let us meet him then. |
| SALISBURY | Or rather, then set forward, for ’twill be<br>Two long days’[[Journey\|journey]], lords, or ere we meet. 20 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Bastard |
| BASTARD | Once more today [[Well\|well]] met, distempered lords.<br>The [[King]] by me requests your presence straight. |
| SALISBURY | The [[King]] hath dispossessed himself of us.<br>We will not line his thin bestainèd cloak<br>With our pure honours, nor attend the foot 25<br>That [[Leaves\|leaves]] the print of [[Blood\|blood]] where’er it walks.<br>Return and tell him so; we know the worst. |
| BASTARD | Whate’er you think, good words I think were best. |
| SALISBURY | Our griefs and not our manners reason now. |
| BASTARD | But there is little reason in your grief.<br>Therefore ’twere reason you had manners now. |
| PEMBROKE | Sir, sir, impatience hath his privilege. |
| BASTARD | ’Tis true—to hurt his master, no man else. |
| SALISBURY | This is the [[Prison\|prison]].<br>He sees [[Arthur]]’s [[Body\|body]]<br><br>What is he lies here? |
| PEMBROKE | O [[Death\|death]], made proud with pure and princely [[Beauty\|beauty]]!<br>The earth had not a hole to hide this deed. 36 |
| SALISBURY | Murder, as hating what himself hath done,<br>Doth lay it open to urge on [[Revenge\|revenge]]. |
| BIGOT | Or when he doomed this [[Beauty\|beauty]] to a [[Grave\|grave]],<br>Found it too precious-princely fora [[Grave\|grave]]. 40 |
| SALISBURY | (to the Bastard)<br>Sir [[Richard]], what think you? You have beheld.<br>Or have you read or heard; or could you think,<br>Or do you almost think, although you see,<br>That you do see? Could thought, without this object,<br>Form such another? This is the very top,<br>The height, the crest, or crest unto the crest,<br>Of murder’s arms; this is the bloodiest shame,<br>The wildest savagery, the vilest stroke<br>That ever wall-eyed wrath or staring rage<br>Presented to the [[Tears\|tears]] of soft remorse. |
| PEMBROKE | All murders past do stand excused in this,<br>And this, so sole and so unmatchable,<br>Shall give a holiness, a purity,<br>To the yet-unbegotten sin of times,<br>And prove a deadly bloodshed but a jest, 55<br>Exampled by this heinous spectacle. |
| BASTARD | It is a damned and a bloody work,<br>The graceless action of a heavy hand—<br>If that it be the work of any hand. |
| SALISBURY | If that it be the work of any hand? 60<br>We had a kind of light what would ensue:<br>It is the shameful work of [[Hubert]]’s hand,<br>The practice and the purpose of the [[King]];<br>From whose obedience I forbid my [[Soul\|soul]],<br>Kneeling before this ruin of sweet life, 65<br>And breathing to his breathless excellence<br>The incense of a vow, a holy vow,<br>Never to taste the pleasures of the world,<br>Never to be infected with delight,<br>Nor conversant with ease and idleness, 70<br>Till I have set a glory to this hand<br>By giving it the worship of [[Revenge\|revenge]]. |
| PEMBROKE | and BIGOT<br>Our souls religiously confirm thy words. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Hubert]] |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Lords, I am hot with haste in seeking you.<br>[[Arthur]] doth live; the [[King]] hath sent for you. |
| SALISBURY | O,he is bold, and blushes not at [[Death\|death]]!—<br>Avaunt, thou hateful [[Villain\|villain]], get thee gone I |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | I am no [[Villain\|villain]]. |
| SALISBURY | Must I rob the law?<br>He draws his [[Sword\|sword]] |
| BASTARD | Your [[Sword\|sword]] is bright, sir; put it up again. |
| SALISBURY | Not till I sheathe it in a murderer’s skin. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | (drawing his [[Sword\|sword]])<br>Stand back, Lord Salisbury, stand back, I say!<br>By heaven, I think my [[Sword\|sword]]’s as sharp as yours.<br>I would not have you, lord, forget yourself,<br>Nor tempt the danger of my true defence,<br>Lest I, by marking of your rage, forget<br>Your worth, your [[Greatness\|greatness]] and nobility. |
| BIGOT | Out, dunghill! Dar’st thou brave a nobleman? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Not for my life; but yet I dare defend<br>My [[Innocent\|innocent]] life against an emperor. |
| SALISBURY | Thou art a murderer. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Do not prove me so;<br>Yet I am none. Whose tongue soe’er speaks false,<br>Not truly speaks; who speaks not truly, lies. |
| PEMBROKE | Cut him to pieces! |
| BASTARD | (drawing his [[Sword\|sword]]) Keep the peace, I say I |
| SALISBURY | Stand by, or I shall gall you, Falconbridge. |
| BASTARD | Thou wert better gall the devil, Salisbury.<br>If thou but frown on me, or stir thy foot,<br>Or teach thy hasty spleen to do me shame,<br>I’ll strike thee dead. Put up thy [[Sword\|sword]] betime,<br>Or I’ll so maul you and your toasting-iron<br>That you shall think the devil is come from hell. 100 |
| BIGOT | What wilt thou do, renowned Falconbridge,<br>Second a [[Villain\|villain]] and a murderer? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Lord Bigot, I am none. |
| BIGOT | Who killed this prince? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | ’Tis not an hour since I left him [[Well\|well]].<br>I honoured him, I loved him, and will weep<br>My date of life out for his sweet life’s loss. |
| SALISBURY | Trust not those cunning waters of his eyes,<br>For villainy is not without such rheum,<br>And he, long traded in it, makes it seem<br>Like rivers of remorse and innocency. II0<br>Away with me, all you whose souls abhor<br>Th’uncleanly savours of a slaughter-[[house]],<br>For I am stifled with this smell of sin. |
| BIGOT | Away toward Bury, to the [[Dauphin]] there. |
| PEMBROKE | There, tell the [[King]], he may enquire us out. 115 |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Pembroke, Salisbury, and Bigot |
| BASTARD | Here’s a good world! Knew you of this fair work?<br>Beyond the infinite and boundless reach<br>Of [[Mercy\|mercy]], if thou didst this deed of [[Death\|death]]<br>Art thou damned, [[Hubert]]. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Do but hear me, sir. |
| BASTARD | Ha! I’ll tell thee what:<br>Thou’rt damned as black—nay nothing is so black—<br>Thou art more deep damned than Prince [[Lucifer]];<br>There is not yet so ugly a fiend of hell<br>As thou shalt be if thou didst kill this [[Child\|child]]. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Upon my [[Soul\|soul]]— |
| BASTARD | If thou didst but consent<br>To this most cruel act, do but [[Despair\|despair]];<br>And if thou want’st a cord, the smallest thread<br>That ever spider twisted from her womb<br>Will serve to strangle thee; a rush will be a beam<br>To hang thee on; or wouldst thou drown thyself,<br>Put but a little [[Water\|water]] in a spoon<br>And it shall be, as all the ocean,<br>Enough to stifle such a [[Villain\|villain]] up.<br>I do suspect thee very grievously. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | If I in act, consent, or sin of thought<br>Be guilty of the stealing that sweet breath<br>Which was embounded in this beauteous clay,<br>Let hell want pains enough to torture me.<br><br>I left him [[Well\|well]]. |
| BASTARD | Go bear him in thine arms.<br>I am amazed, methinks, and lose my way<br>Among the thorns and dangers of this world.<br>[[Hubert]] takes up [[Arthur]] in his arms<br>How easy dost thou take all England up I<br>From forth this morsel of dead royalty,<br>The life, the right, and [[Truth\|truth]] of all this realm<br>Is fled to heaven, and England now is left<br>To tug and scramble, and to part by th’ teeth<br>The unowed interest of proud swelling state.<br>Now for the bare-picked bone of majesty<br>Doth dogged war bristle his angry crest,<br>And snarleth in the gentle eyes of peace;<br>Now powers from [[Home\|home]] and discontents at [[Home\|home]]<br>Meet in one line, and vast confusion waits,<br>As doth a raven on a sick-fall’n beast,<br>The imminent [[Decay\|decay]] of wrested pomp. 155<br>Now happy he whose cloak and cincture can<br>Hold out this tempest. Bear away that [[Child\|child]],<br>And follow me with [[Speed\|speed]]. I’ll to the [[King]].<br>A thousand businesses are brief in hand,<br>And heaven itself doth frown upon the land. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |
| *(didascalia)* | 5.1 Flourish.Enter [[King John]] and Cardinal Pandolf, with attendants |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King John\|KING JOHN]] giving Pandolf the [[Crown\|crown]] |
| BASTARD | Thus have I yielded up into your hand<br>The circle of my glory.<br>PANDOLF(giving back the [[Crown\|crown]])Take again<br>From this my hand, as holding of the Pope,<br>Your sovereign [[Greatness\|greatness]] and authority. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Now keep your holy word: go meet the French,<br>And from his Holiness use all your power<br>To stop their marches ‘fore we are enflamed.<br>Our discontented counties do revolt,<br>Our people quarrel with obedience,<br>Swearing allegiance and the [[Love\|love]] of [[Soul\|soul]]<br>To stranger [[Blood\|blood]], to foreign royalty.<br>This inundation of mistempered [[Humour\|humour]]<br>Rests by you only to be qualified.<br>Then pause not, for the present [[Time\|time]]’s so sick<br>That present med’cine must be ministered,<br>Or overthrow incurable ensues. |
| PANDOLF | It was my breath that blew this tempest up,<br>Upon your stubborn usage of the Pope,<br>But since you are a gentle convertite,<br>My tongue shall hush again this [[Storm\|storm]] of war<br>And make fair weather in your blust’ring land.<br>On this Ascension Day, remember [[Well\|well]],<br>Upon your oath of service to the Pope,<br>Go I to make the French lay down their arms. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[King John]] |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Is this Ascension Day Did not the [[Prophet\|prophet]]<br>Say that before Ascension Day at [[Noon\|noon]]<br>My [[Crown\|crown]] I should give off? Even so I have.<br>I did suppose it should be on constraint,<br>But, heaven be thanked, it is but voluntary. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Bastard |
| BASTARD | All [[Kent]] hath yielded; nothing there holds out<br>But Dover [[Castle]]. [[London]] hath received,<br>Like a kind host, the [[Dauphin]] and his powers.<br>Your nobles will not hear you, but are gone<br>To offer service to your enemy;<br>And wild amazement hurries up and down<br>The little number of your doubtful friends. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Would not my lords return to me again<br>After they heard young [[Arthur]] was alive? |
| BASTARD | They found him dead and cast into the streets,<br>An empty casket, where the [[Jewel\|jewel]] of life<br>By some damned hand was robbed and ta’en away. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | That [[Villain\|villain]] [[Hubert]] told me he did live. |
| BASTARD | Soon my [[Soul\|soul]] he did, for aught he knew.<br>But wherefore do you droop? Why look you sad?<br>Be great in act as you have been in thought.<br>Let not the world see [[Fear\|fear]] and sad distrust<br>Govern the motion of a kingly eye.<br>Be stirring as the [[Time\|time]], be [[Fire\|fire]] with [[Fire\|fire]];<br>Threaten the threat’ner, and outface the brow<br>Of bragging horror. So shall inferior eyes,<br>That borrow their behaviours from the great,<br>Grow great by your example, and put on<br>The dauntless spirit of resolution.<br>Away, and glisten like the god of war<br>When he intendeth to become the field.<br>Show boldness and aspiring confidence.<br>What, shall they seek the [[Lion\|lion]] in his den<br>And fright him there, and make him tremble there?<br>O, let it not be said I Forage, and run<br>To meet displeasure farther from the doors,<br>And grapple with him ere he come so nigh. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | The legate of the Pope hath been with me,<br>And I have made a happy peace with him,<br>And he hath promised to dismiss the powers<br>Led by the [[Dauphin]]. |
| BASTARD | O inglorious league!<br>Shall we, upon the footing of our land,<br>Send fair-play orders, and make compromise,<br>Insinuation, parley, and base truce<br>To arms invasive? Shall a beardless boy,<br>A cockered silken wanton, brave our fields<br>And flesh his spirit in a warlike soil,<br>Mocking the air with colours idly spread,<br>And find no check? Let us, my liege, to arms!<br>Perchance the Cardinal cannot make your peace,<br>Or if he do, let it at least be said<br>They saw we had a purpose of defence. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Have thou the ordering of this present [[Time\|time]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Away, then, with good [[Courage\|courage]]! AsideYet I know |
| BASTARD | Our party may [[Well\|well]] meet a prouder foe. Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter, marching in arms, Louis the [[Dauphin]], the Earl off Salisbury, Count Melun, the Earl of Pembroke, and Lord Bigot, with soldiers |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | My Lord Melun, let this be copied out,<br>And keep it safe for our remembrance.<br>Return the precedent to these lords again,<br>That having our fair order written down,<br>Both they and we, perusing o’er these notes,<br>May know wherefore we took the sacrament<br>And keep our faiths firm and inviolable. |
| SALISBURY | Upon our sides it never shall be broken.<br>And, noble [[Dauphin]], albeit we swear<br>A voluntary zealand an unurgéd [[Faith\|faith]]<br>To your proceedings, yet believe me, Prince,<br>I am not glad that such a sore of [[Time\|time]]<br>Should seek a plaster by contemned revolt,<br>And heal the inveterate canker of one wound<br>By making many. O,it grieves my [[Soul\|soul]]<br>That I must draw this metal from my side<br>To be a widow-maker! O, and there<br>Where honourable rescue and defence<br>Cries out upon the name of Salisbury I<br>But such is the infection of the [[Time\|time]],<br>That for the health and physic of our right,<br>We cannot deal but with the very hand<br>Of stern injustice and confused wrong.<br>And is’t not pity, O my grieved friends,<br>That we the sons and children of this isle<br>Was born to see so sad an hour as this,<br>Wherein we step after a stranger, march<br>Upon her gentle bosom, and fill up<br>Her enemies’ ranks? I must withdraw and weep<br>Upon the spot of this enforced cause—<br>To grace the gentry of a land remote,<br>And follow unacquainted colours here.<br>What, here? O nation, that thou couldst remove;<br>That Neptune’s arms who clippeth thee about<br>Would bear thee from the knowledge of thyself<br>And gripple thee unto a pagan shore,<br>Where these two Christian armies might combine<br>The [[Blood\|blood]] of malice in a vein of league,<br>And not to spend it so unneighbourly. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | A noble temper dost thou show in this,<br>And great affections, wrestling in thy bosom,<br>Doth make an earthquake of nobility.<br>O, what a noble combat hast thou fought<br>Between compulsion and a brave respect!<br>Let me wipe off this honourable dew<br>That silverly doth progress on thy cheeks.<br>My heart hath melted at a lady’s [[Tears\|tears]],<br>Being an ordinary inundation;<br>But this effusion of such manly drops,<br>This shower blown up by tempest of the [[Soul\|soul]],<br>Startles mine eyes, and makes me more amazed<br>Than had I seen the vaulty top of heaven<br>Figured quite o’er with burning meteors.<br>Lift up thy brow, renowned Salisbury,<br>And with a great heart heave away this [[Storm\|storm]]; 55<br>Commend these waters to those baby eyes<br>That never saw the giant world enraged,<br>Nor met with Fortune other than at feasts,<br>Full warm of [[Blood\|blood]], of mirth, of gossiping.<br>Come, come, for thou shalt thrust thy hand as deep<br>Into the purse of rich prosperity<br>As Louis himself. So, nobles, shall you all<br>That knit your sinews to the strength of mine. |
| *(didascalia)* | A trumpet sounds |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | And even there methinks an angel spake! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Cardinal Pandolf |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | Look where the holy legate comes apace,<br>To give us warrant from the hand of heaven,<br>And on our actions set the name of right<br>With holy breath. |
| PANDOLF | Hail, noble prince of France!<br>The next is this. [[King John]] hath reconciled<br>Himself to Rome; his spirit is come in<br>That so stood out against the Holy Church,<br>The great metropolis and See of Rome;<br>Therefore thy threat’ning colours now [[Wind\|wind]] up,<br>And tame the savage spirit of wild war,<br>That like a [[Lion\|lion]] fostered up at hand<br>It may lie gently at the foot of peace,<br>And be no further harmful than in show. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | Your grace shall pardon me: I will not back.<br>I am too high-born to be propertied,<br>To be a secondary at control,<br>Or useful serving-man and instrument<br>To any sovereign state throughout the world.<br>Your breath first kindled the dead coal of wars<br>Between this chastised kingdom and myself,<br>And brought in matter that should feed this [[Fire\|fire]];<br>And now ‘tis far too huge to be blown out<br>With that same weak [[Wind\|wind]] which enkindled it.<br>You taught me how to know the face of right,<br>Acquainted me with interest to this land,<br>Yea, thrust this enterprise into my heart;<br>And come ye now to tell me John hath made<br>His peace with Rome? What is that peace to me?<br>I, by the [[Honour\|honour]] of my [[Marriage\|marriage]] bed,<br>After young [[Arthur]], claim this land for mine;<br>And now it is half conquered, must I back<br>Because that John hath made his peace with Rome?<br>Am I Rome’s slave? What penny hath Rome borne,<br>What men provided, what munition sent<br>To underprop this action? Is’t not I<br>That undergo this charge? Who else but I,<br>And such as to my claim are liable,<br>Sweat in this business and maintain this war?<br>Have I not heard these islanders shout out<br>‘Vive le Roi!’as I have banked their towns?<br>Have I not here the best cards for the game, 105<br>To win this easy match played for a [[Crown\|crown]]?<br>And shall I now give o’er the yielded set?<br>No, no, on my [[Soul\|soul]], it never shall be said. |
| PANDOLF | You look but on the outside of this work. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | Outside or inside, I will not return<br>Till my attempt so much be glorified<br>As to my ample [[Hope\|hope]] was promised<br>Before I drew this gallant head of war,<br>And culled these fiery spirits from the world<br>To outlook conquest and to win renown<br>Even in the jaws of danger and of [[Death\|death]].<br>A trumpet sounds<br>What lusty trumpet thus doth summon us? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Bastard |
| BASTARD | According to the fair play of the world,<br>Let me have audience; I am sent to speak.<br>My holy lord of Milan, from the [[King]]<br>I come to learn how you have dealt for him,<br>And as you answer I do know the scope<br>And warrant limited unto my tongue. |
| PANDOLF | The [[Dauphin]] is too wilful-opposite,<br>And will not temporize with my entreaties.<br>He flatly says he’ll not lay down his arms. |
| BASTARD | By all the [[Blood\|blood]] that ever fury breathed,<br>The youth says [[Well\|well]]. Now hear our English [[King\|king]],<br>For thus his royalty doth speak in me.<br>He is prepared, and reason too he should.<br>This apish and unmannerly approach,<br>This harnessed [[Masque\|masque]] and unadvised revel,<br>This unhaired sauciness and boyish troops,<br>The [[King]] doth smile at, and is [[Well\|well]] prepared<br>To whip this dwarfish war, these pigmy arms, 135<br>From out the circle of his territories.<br>That hand which had the strength even at your door<br>To cudgel you and make you take the hatch,<br>To dive like buckets in concealed wells,<br>To crouch in litter of your stable planks,<br>To lie like pawns locked up in chests and trunks,<br>To hug with swine, to seek sweet safety out<br>In vaults and prisons, and to thrill and shake<br>Even at the crying of your nation’s crow,<br>Thinking his voice an armed Englishman;<br>Shall that victorious hand be feebled here<br>That in your chambers gave you chastisement?<br>No! Know the gallant monarch is in arms,<br>And like an eagle o’er his eyrie towers<br>To souse annoyance that comes near his nest.<br>(To the English lords)<br>And you degenerate, you ingrate revolts,<br>You bloody Neros, ripping up the womb<br>Of your dear mother England, blush for shame;<br>For your own ladies and pale-visaged maids<br>Like Amazons come tripping after drums;<br>Their thimbles into armed gauntlets change,<br>Their needles to lances, and their gentle hearts<br>To fierce and bloody inclination. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | There end thy brave, and turn thy face in peace.<br>We grant thou canst outscold us. Fare thee [[Well\|well]]: 160<br>We hold our [[Time\|time]] too precious to be spent<br>With such a brabbler. |
| PANDOLF | Give me leave to speak. |
| BASTARD | No, I will speak.<br>LouisTHE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] We will attend to neither.—<br>Strike up the drums, and let the tongue of war<br>Plead for our interest and our being here. |
| BASTARD | Indeed your drums, being beaten, will cry out;<br>And so shall you, being beaten. Do but start<br>An echo with the clamour of thy drum,<br>And even at hand a drum is ready braced<br>That shall reverberate all as loud as thine. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound but another, and another shall |
| BASTARD | As loud as thine rattle the welkin’s ear,<br>And mock the deep-mouthed thunder; for at hand,<br>Not trusting to this halting legate here,<br>Whom he hath used rather for sport than need,<br>Is warlike John; and in his forehead sits<br>A bare-ribbed [[Death]], whose office is this day<br>To feast upon whole thousands of the French. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | Strike up our drums to find this danger out. |
| BASTARD | And thou shalt find it, [[Dauphin]], do not [[Doubt\|doubt]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Drums beat. Exeunt the Bastard Fat onedoor, all the rest, marching, at another door |

### Atto 5, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum. Enter [[King John]] Fat one door and [[Hubert]] at another door |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | How goes the day with us? O, tell me, [[Hubert]]. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Badly, I [[Fear\|fear]]. How fares your majesty? |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | This fever that hath troubled me so long<br>Lies heavy on me. O, my heart is sick! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | My lord, your valiant kinsman Falconbridge<br>Desires your majesty to leave the field,<br>And send him word by me which way you go. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Tell him toward Swineshead, to the abbey there. |
| MESSENGER | Be of good comfort, for the great supply<br>That was expected by the [[Dauphin]] here<br>Are wrecked three nights ago on Goodwin Sands.<br>This news was brought to [[Richard]], but even now<br>The French fight coldly and retire themselves. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Ay me, this [[Tyrant\|tyrant]] fever burns me up,<br>And will not let me welcome this good news.<br>Set on toward Swineshead. To my litter straight;<br>Weakness possesseth me, and I am faint. Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | 5.4 Alarum.Enter the Earls of Salisbury and Pembroke, and Lord Bigot |
| SALISBURY | I did not think the [[King]] so stored with friends. |
| PEMBROKE | Up once again; put spirit in the French.<br>If they miscarry, we miscarry too. |
| SALISBURY | That misbegotten devil Falconbridge,<br>In spite of spite, [[Alone\|alone]] upholds the day. |
| PEMBROKE | They say [[King John]], sore sick, hath left the field. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Count Melun, wounded, led by a soldier |
| MELUN | Lead me to the revolts of England here. |
| SALISBURY | When we were happy, we had other names. |
| PEMBROKE | It is the Count Melun. |
| SALISBURY | Wounded to [[Death\|death]]. |
| MELUN | Fly, noble English, you are bought and sold.<br>Unthread the rude eye of rebellion,<br>And welcome [[Home\|home]] again discarded [[Faith\|faith]];<br>Seek out [[King John]] and fall before his feet,<br>For if the French be lords of this loud day<br>He means to recompense the pains you take<br>By cutting off your heads. Thus hath he sworn,<br>And I with him, and many more with me,<br>Upon the altar at Saint Edmundsbury,<br>Even on that altar where we swore to you<br>Dear amity and everlasting [[Love\|love]]. |
| SALISBURY | May this be possible? May this be true? |
| MELUN | Have I not hideous [[Death\|death]] within my view,<br>Retaining but a quantity of life,<br>Which bleeds away, even as a form of wax<br>Resolveth from his figure ‘gainst the [[Fire\|fire]]?<br>What in the world should make me now deceive,<br>Since I must lose the use of all deceit?<br>Why should I then be false, since it is true<br>That I must die here, and live hence by [[Truth\|truth]]?<br>I say again, if Louis do win the day,<br>He is forsworn if e’er those eyes of yours<br>Behold another daybreak in the east;<br>But even this [[Night\|night]], whose black contagious breath<br>Already smokes about the burning cresset<br>Of the old, feeble, and day-wearied sun,<br>Even this ill [[Night\|night]] your breathing shall expire,<br>Paying the fine of rated treachery<br>Even with a treacherous fine of all your lives,<br>If Louis by your assistance win the day.<br>Commend me to one [[Hubert]] with your [[King\|king]].<br>The [[Love\|love]] of him, and this respect besides,<br>For that my grandsire was an Englishman,<br>Awakes my [[Conscience\|conscience]] to confess all this;<br>In lieu whereof, I pray you bear me hence<br>From forth the noise and rumour of the field,<br>Where I may think the remnant of my thoughts<br>In peace, and part this [[Body\|body]] and my [[Soul\|soul]]<br>With contemplation and devout desires. |
| SALISBURY | We do believe thee; and beshrew my [[Soul\|soul]]<br>But I do [[Love\|love]] the favour and the form<br>Of this most fair occasion; by the which<br>We will untread the steps of damned flight,<br>And like a bated and retired flood,<br>Leaving our rankness and irregular course,<br>Stoop low within those bounds we have o’erlooked,<br>And calmly run on in obedience<br>Even to our ocean, to our great [[King John]].<br>My arm shall give thee help to bear thee hence,<br>For I do see the cruel pangs of [[Death\|death]]<br>Right in thine eye.—Away,my friends! New flight,<br>And happy newness that intends old right. Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | 5.5 Alarum,. retreat. Enter Louis the [[Dauphin]], and his train |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | The sun of heaven, methought, was loath to set,<br>But stayed and made the western welkin blush,<br>When English measured backward their own ground<br>In faint retire. O, bravely came we off,<br>When with a volley of our needless shot,<br>After such bloody toil, we bid good [[Night\|night]],<br>And wound our tatt’ring colours clearly up,<br>Last in the field and almost lords of it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | Where is my prince the [[Dauphin]]? |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | Here. What news? |
| MESSENGER | The Count Melun is slain; the English lords<br>By his [[Persuasion\|persuasion]] are again fall’n off;<br>And your supply which you have wished so long<br>Are cast away and sunk on Goodwin Sands. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | Ah, foul shrewd news! Beshrew thy very heart<br>I did not think to be so sad tonight<br>As this hath made me. Who was he that said<br>[[King John]] did fly an hour or two before<br>The stumbling [[Night\|night]] did part our weary powers? |
| MESSENGER | Whoever spoke it, it is true, my lord. |
| LOUIS THE [[Dauphin\|DAUPHIN]] | [[Well]], keep good quarter and good care tonight.<br>The day shall not be up so soon as I,<br>To try the fair adventure of tomorrow. Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Bastard With a lightand [[Hubert]] With a [[Pistol\|pistol]], severally |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Who’s there? Speak, ho! Speak quickly, or I shoot. |
| BASTARD | A friend. What art thou? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Of the part of England. |
| BASTARD | Whither dost thou go? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | What’s that to thee?<br>Why may not I demand of thine affairs<br>As [[Well\|well]] as thou of mine? |
| BASTARD | [[Hubert]], I think. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Thou hast a perfect thought.<br>I will upon all hazards [[Well\|well]] believe<br>Thou art my friend that know’st my tongue so [[Well\|well]].<br>Who art thou? |
| BASTARD | Who thou wilt. An if thou please,<br>Thou mayst befriend me so much as to think<br>I come one way of the Plantagenets. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Unkind remembrance! Thou and eyeless [[Night\|night]]<br>Have done me shame. Brave soldier, pardon me<br>That any accent breaking from thy tongue<br>Should ’scape the true acquaintance of mine ear. |
| BASTARD | Come, come, sans compliment. What news abroad? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Why, here walk I in the black brow of [[Night\|night]]<br>To find you out. |
| BASTARD | Brief, then, and what’s the news? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | O my sweet sir, news fitting to the [[Night\|night]]:<br>Black, fearful, comfortless, and horrible. |
| BASTARD | Show me the very wound of this ill news;<br>I am no woman, I’ll not swoon at it. |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | The [[King]], I [[Fear\|fear]], is poisoned by a monk.<br>I left him almost speechless, and broke out<br>To acquaint you with this evil, that you might<br>The better arm you to the sudden [[Time\|time]]<br>Than if you had at leisure known of this. |
| BASTARD | How did he take it? Who did taste to him? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | A monk, I tell you, a resolved [[Villain\|villain]],<br>Whose bowels suddenly burst out. The [[King]]<br>Yet speaks, and peradventure may recover. |
| BASTARD | Who didst thou leave to tend his majesty? |
| [[Hubert\|HUBERT]] | Why, know you not? The lords are all come back,<br>And brought Prince Henry in their company,<br>At whose request the [[King]] hath pardoned them,<br>And they are all about his majesty. |
| BASTARD | Withhold thine indignation, mighty heaven,<br>And tempt us not to bear above our power.<br>I’ll tell thee, [[Hubert]], half my power this [[Night\|night]],<br>Passing these flats, are taken by the tide.<br>These [[Lincoln]] Washes have devoured them;<br>Myself, [[Well\|well]] mounted, hardly have escaped.<br>Away before! Conduct me to the [[King]].<br>I [[Doubt\|doubt]] he will be dead or ere I come. Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 7

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prince Henry, the [[Earl of Salisbury]], and Lord Bigot |
| PRINCE HENRY | It is too late. The life of all his [[Blood\|blood]]<br>Is touched corruptibly, and his pure brain,<br>Which some suppose the [[Soul\|soul]]’s frail dwelling-[[house]],<br>Doth by the idle comments that it makes<br>Foretell the ending of [[Mortality\|mortality]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of Pembroke |
| PEMBROKE | His highness yet doth speak, and holds belief<br>That being brought into the open air,<br>It would allay the burning quality<br>Of that fell [[Poison\|poison]] which assaileth him. |
| PRINCE HENRY | Let him be brought into the orchard here.— |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Lord Bigot |
| PRINCE HENRY | Doth he still rage? |
| PEMBROKE | He is more patient<br>Than when you left him. Even now, he sung. |
| PRINCE HENRY | O, [[Vanity\|vanity]] of sickness! Fierce extremes<br>In their continuance will not feel themselves.<br>[[Death]], having preyed upon the outward parts,<br>[[Leaves]] them invincible, and his siege is now<br>Against the mind; the which he pricks and wounds<br>With many legions of strange fantasies,<br>Which in their throng and press to that last hold<br>Confound themselves. ’Tis strange that [[Death\|death]] should<br>sing.<br>I am the cygnet to this pale faint swan,<br>Who chants a doleful [[Hymn\|hymn]] to his own [[Death\|death]],<br>And from the organ-pipe of frailty sings<br>His [[Soul\|soul]] and [[Body\|body]] to their lasting rest. |
| SALISBURY | Be of good comfort, Prince, for you are born<br>To set a form upon that indigest<br>Which he hath left so shapeless and so rude. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King John]] is brought in, with Lord Bigot attending |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Ay marry, now my [[Soul\|soul]] hath elbow-room;<br>It would not out at windows nor at doors.<br>There is so hot a [[Summer\|summer]] in my bosom<br>That all my bowels crumble up to [[Dust\|dust]];<br>I am a scribbled form, drawn with a pen<br>Upon a parchment, and against this [[Fire\|fire]]<br>Do I shrink up. |
| PRINCE HENRY | How fares your majesty? |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Poisoned, ill fare! Dead, forsook, cast off;<br>And none of you will bid the [[Winter\|winter]] come<br>To thrust his icy fingers in my maw,<br>Nor let my kingdom’s rivers take their course<br>Through my burned bosom, nor entreat the north<br>To make his bleak winds kiss my parchèd lips 40<br>And comfort me with cold. I do not ask you much;<br>I beg cold comfort, and you are so strait<br>And so ingrateful you deny me that. |
| PRINCE HENRY | O, that there were some virtue in my [[Tears\|tears]]<br>That might relieve you! |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | The salt in them is hot. |
| *(didascalia)* | Within me is a hell, and there the [[Poison\|poison]] |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Is, as a fiend, confined to tyrannize<br>On unreprievable condemned [[Blood\|blood]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Bastard |
| BASTARD | O, I am scalded with my violent motion<br>And spleen of [[Speed\|speed]] to see your majesty! |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | O cousin, thou art come to set mine eye.<br>The tackle of my heart is cracked and burnt,<br>And all the shrouds wherewith my life should sail<br>Are turned to one thread, one little hair;<br>My heart hath one poor string to stay it by,<br>Which holds but till thy news be uttered,<br>And then all this thou seest is but a clod<br>And module of confounded royalty. |
| BASTARD | The [[Dauphin]] is preparing hitherward,<br>Where God He knows how we shall answer him;<br>For in a [[Night\|night]] the best part of my power,<br>As I upon advantage did remove,<br>Were in the Washes all unwarily<br>Devoured by the unexpected flood.<br>[[King John]] dies |
| SALISBURY | You breathe these dead news in as dead an ear.<br>(To [[King John]]) My liege, my lord!—But now a [[King\|king]],<br>now thus. |
| PRINCE HENRY | Even so must I run on, and even so stop.<br>What surety of the world, what [[Hope\|hope]], what stay,<br>When this was now a [[King\|king]] and now is clay? |
| BASTARD | (to [[King John]])<br>Art thou gone so? I do but stay behind<br>To do the office for thee of [[Revenge\|revenge]],<br>And then my [[Soul\|soul]] shall wait on thee to heaven,<br>As it on earth hath been thy servant still.<br>(To the lords) Now, now, you [[Stars\|stars]] that move in your<br>right spheres,<br>Where be your powers? Show now your mended<br>faiths,<br>And instantly return with me again,<br>To push destruction and perpetual shame<br>Out of the weak door of our fainting land.<br>Straight let us seek, or straight we shall be sought.<br>The [[Dauphin]] rages at our very heels. |
| SALISBURY | It seems you know not, then, so much as we.<br>The Cardinal Pandolf is within at rest,<br>Who half an hour since came from the [[Dauphin]],<br>And brings from him such offers of our peace<br>As we with [[Honour\|honour]] and respect may take,<br>With purpose presently to leave this war. |
| BASTARD | He will the rather do it when he sees<br>Ourselves [[Well\|well]]-sinewed to our own defence. |
| SALISBURY | Nay, ’tis in a manner done already,<br>For many carriages he hath dispatched<br>To the sea-side, and put his cause and quarrel<br>To the disposing of the Cardinal,<br>With whom yourself, myself, and other lords,<br>If you think meet, this afternoon will post<br>To consummate this business happily. |
| BASTARD | Let it be so.—And you, my noble prince,<br>With other princes that may best be spared,<br>Shall wait upon your father’s funeral. |
| PRINCE HENRY | At Worcester must his [[Body\|body]] be interred,<br>For so he willed it. |
| BASTARD | Thither shall it then, 100<br>And happily may your sweet [[Self\|self]] put on<br>The lineal state and glory of the land,<br>To whom with all submission, on my knee,<br>I do bequeath my faithful services<br>And true subjection everlastingly. 105<br>He kneels |
| SALISBURY | And the like tender of our [[Love\|love]] we make,<br>To rest without a spot for evermore.<br>Salisbury, Pembroke and Bigot kneel |
| PRINCE HENRY | I have a kind of [[Soul\|soul]] that would give thanks,<br>And knows not how to do it but with [[Tears\|tears]].<br>He weeps |
| *(didascalia)* | BASTARD rising |
| PRINCE HENRY | O, let us pay the [[Time\|time]] but needful woe,<br>Since it hath been beforehand with our griefs.<br>This England never did, nor never shall,<br>Lie at the proud foot of a conqueror<br>But when it first did help to wound itself.<br>Now these her princes are come [[Home\|home]] again,<br>Come the three corners of the world in arms<br>And we shall shock them. Naught shall make us rue<br>If England to itself do rest but true. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Exeunt with the [[Body\|body]] |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_1" data-title="King John — Act I, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/child,archetype/king,motif/blood,motif/death,motif/decay,motif/desire,motif/lightning,motif/lion,motif/love,motif/night,motif/poison,motif/river,motif/rose,motif/silence,motif/soul,motif/time,motif/well,motif/window,concept/conscience,concept/conversion,concept/doubt,concept/honour,concept/hope,concept/mercy,concept/truth,setting/court,setting/germany,character/constance,character/eleanor,character/king_john,character/king_of_france,character/philip_the_bastard,character/queen,character/richard,character/speed"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | [Flourish.] Enter [[King John]], [[Queen]] [[Eleanor]], and the Earls of Pembroke, Essex, and Salisbury; with them Châtillon of France |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Now say, Châtillon, what would France with us?<br>CHÂTILLON<br>Thus, after greeting, speaks the [[King of France]],<br>In my behaviour, to the majesty—<br>The borrowed majesty—of England here. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | A strange beginning: ‘borrowed majesty’? |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | [[Silence]], good mother, hear the embassy.<br>CHÂTILLON<br>Philip of France, in right and true behalf<br>Of thy deceased brother Geoffrey’s son,<br>[[arthur\|Arthur]] Plantagenet, lays most lawful claim<br><br>To this fair island and the territories,<br>To Ireland, Poitou, Anjou, Touraine, Maine;<br>Desiring thee to lay aside the [[Sword\|sword]]<br>Which sways usurpingly these several titles,<br>And put the same into young [[arthur\|Arthur]]’s hand,<br>Thy nephew and right royal sovereign. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | What follows if we disallow of this?<br>CHÂTILLON<br>The proud control of fierce and bloody war,<br>To enforce these rights so forcibly withheld— |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Here have we war for war, and [[Blood\|blood]] for [[Blood\|blood]],<br>Controlment for controlment: so answer France.<br>CHÂTILLON<br>Then take my [[King\|king]]’s defiance from my mouth,<br>The farthest limit of my embassy. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Bear mine to him, and so depart in peace.<br>Be thou as [[Lightning\|lightning]] in the eyes of France,<br>For ere thou canst report, I will be there; 25<br>The thunder of my cannon shall be heard.<br>So hence. Be thou the trumpet of our wrath,<br>And sullen presage of your own [[Decay\|decay]].—<br>An honourable conduct let him have;<br>Pembroke, look to’t.—Farewell, Châtillon. 30 |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Châtillon and Pembroke |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | What now, my son? Have I not ever said<br>How that ambitious [[Constance]] would not cease<br>Till she had kindled France and all the world<br>Upon the right and party of her son ?<br>This might have been prevented and made whole 35<br>With very easy arguments of [[Love\|love]],<br>Which now the manage of two kingdoms must<br>With fearful-bloody issue arbitrate. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Our strong possession and our right for us. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | (aside to [[King John]])<br>Your strong possession much more than your right,<br>Or else it must go wrong with you and me: 41<br>So much my [[Conscience\|conscience]] whispers in your ear,<br>Which none but heaven and you and I shall hear. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Sheriff, [who whispers to Essex] |
| ESSEX | My liege, here is the strangest controversy,<br>Come from the country to be judged by you, 45<br>That e’er I heard. Shall I produce the men? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[King John\|KING JOHN]] Let them approach.—Exit Sheriff |
| ESSEX | Our abbeys and our priories shall pay<br>This expeditious charge. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Robert Falconbridge and [[Philip the Bastard]] With |
| ESSEX | the |
| *(didascalia)* | Sheriff |
| ESSEX | What men are you? |
| BASTARD | Your faithful subject I, a gentleman 50<br>Born in Northamptonshire, and eldest son,<br>As I suppose, to Robert Falconbridge,<br>A soldier, by the [[Honour\|honour]]-giving hand<br>Of Cœur-de-[[Lion\|lion]] knighted in the field. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | What art thou? 55 |
| FALCONBRIDGE | The son and heir to that same Falconbridge. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Is that the elder, and art thou the heir?<br>You came not of one mother then, it seems. |
| BASTARD | Most certain of one mother, mighty [[King]]—<br>That is [[Well\|well]] known—and, as I think, one father. 60<br>But for the certain knowledge of that [[Truth\|truth]]<br>I put you o’er to heaven, and to my mother.<br>Of that I [[Doubt\|doubt]] as all men’s children may. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | Out on thee, rude man ! Thou dost shame thy mother<br>And wound her [[Honour\|honour]] with this diffidence. 65 |
| BASTARD | I, Madam? No, I have no reason for it.<br>That is my brother’s plea and none of mine,<br>The which if he can prove, a pops me out<br>At least from fair five hundred pound a year.<br>Heaven guard my mother’s [[Honour\|honour]], and my land! 70 |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | A good blunt fellow.—Why, being younger born,<br>Doth he lay claim to thine inheritance ? |
| BASTARD | I know not why, except to get the land;<br>But once he slandered me with bastardy.<br>But whe’er I be as true begot or no, 75<br>That still I lay upon my mother’s head;<br>But that I am as [[Well\|well]] begot, my liege—<br>Fair fall the bones that took the pains for me—<br>Compare our faces and be judge yourself.<br>If old Sir Robert did beget us both 80<br>And were our father, and this son like him,<br>O old Sir Robert, father, on my knee<br>I give heaven thanks I was not like to thee. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Why, what a madcap hath heaven lent us here! |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | He hath a trick of Coeur-de-[[Lion\|lion]]’s face; 85<br>The accent of his tongue affecteth him.<br>Do you not read some tokens of my son<br>In the large composition of this man? |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Mine eye hath [[Well\|well]] examined his parts,<br>And finds them perfect [[Richard]].<br>(To Robert Falconbridge) Sirrah, speak: 90<br>What doth move you to claim your brother’s land? |
| BASTARD | Because he hath a half-face like my father!<br>With half that face would he have all my land,<br>A half-faced groat five hundred pound a year. |
| FALCONBRIDGE | My gracious liege, when that my father lived, 95<br>Your brother did employ my father much— |
| BASTARD | [[Well]], sir, by this you cannot get my land.<br>Your tale must be how he employed my mother. |
| FALCONBRIDGE | And once dispatched him in an embassy<br>To [[Germany]], there with the Emperor<br>To treat of high affairs touching that [[Time\|time]].<br>Th‘advantage of his absence took the [[King]],<br>And in the meantime sojourned at my father’s,<br>Where how he did prevail I shame to speak.<br>But [[Truth\|truth]] is [[Truth\|truth]]:large lengths of seas and shores<br>Between my father and my mother lay, 106<br>As I have heard my father speak himself,<br>When this same lusty gentleman was got.<br>Upon his deathbed he by will bequeathed<br>His lands to me, and took it on his [[Death\|death]] 110<br>That this my mother’s son was none of his;<br>And if he were, he came into the world<br>Full fourteen weeks before the course of [[Time\|time]].<br>Then, good my liege, let me have what is mine,<br>My father’s land, as was my father’s will. 115 |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Sirrah, your brother is legitimate.<br>Your father’s wife did after wedlock bear him,<br>And if she did play false, the fault was hers,<br>Which fault lies on the hazards of all husbands<br>That marry wives. Tell me, how if my brother,<br>Who, as you say, took pains to get this son,<br>Had of your father claimed this son for his ?<br>In sooth, good friend, your father might have kept<br>This calf, bred from his cow, from all the world;<br>In sooth he might. Then if he were my brother’s, 125<br>My brother might not claim him, nor your father,<br>Being none of his, refuse him. This concludes:<br>My mother’s son did get your father’s heir;<br>Your father’s heir must have your father’s land. |
| FALCONBRIDGE | Shall then my father’s will be of no force 130<br>To dispossess that [[Child\|child]] which is not his? |
| BASTARD | Of no more force to dispossess me, sir,<br>Than was his will to get me, as I think. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | Whether hadst thou rather be: a Falconbridge,<br>And like thy brother to enjoy thy land, 135<br>Or the reputed son of Cœur-de-[[Lion\|lion]],<br>Lord of thy presence, and no land beside? |
| BASTARD | Madam, an if my brother had my shape,<br>And I had his, Sir Robert’s his like him,<br>And if my legs were two such riding-rods, 140<br>My arms such eel-skins stuffed, my face so thin<br>That in mine ear I durst not stick a [[Rose\|rose]]<br>Lest men should say ‘Look where three-farthings<br>goes!’,<br>And, to his shape, were heir to all this land,<br>Would I might never stir from off this place.<br>I would give it every foot to have this face;<br>It would not be Sir Nob in any case. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | I like thee [[Well\|well]]. Wilt thou forsake thy fortune,<br>Bequeath thy land to him, and follow me?<br>I am a soldier and now bound to France. 150 |
| BASTARD | Brother, take you my land; I’ll take my chance.<br>Your face hath got five hundred pound a year,<br>Yet sell your face for fivepence and ’tis dear.—<br>Madam, I’ll follow you unto the [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | Nay, I would have you go before me thither. 155 |
| BASTARD | Our country manners give our betters way. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | What is thy name? |
| BASTARD | Philip, my liege, so is my name begun:<br>Philip, good old Sir Robert’s wife’s eldest son, |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | From henceforth bear his name whose form thou<br>bear’st. 160<br>Kneel thou down Philip, but arise more great:<br>He knights the Bastard<br>Arise Sir [[Richard]] and Plantagenet. |
| BASTARD | Brother by th’ mother’s side, give me your hand.<br>My father gave me [[Honour\|honour]], yours gave land.<br>Now blessèd be the hour, by [[Night\|night]] or day, 165<br>When I was got, Sir Robert was away. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] [[Eleanor\|ELEANOR]] | The very spirit of Plantagenet I<br>I am thy grandam, [[Richard]]; call me so. |
| BASTARD | Madam, by chance, but not by [[Truth\|truth]]; what though?<br>Something about, a little from the right,<br>In at the [[Window\|window]], or else o‘er the hatch;<br>Who dares not stir by day must walk by [[Night\|night]],<br>And have is have, however men do catch.<br>Near or far off, [[Well\|well]] won is still [[Well\|well]] shot,<br>And I am I, howe’er I was begot. |
| [[King John\|KING JOHN]] | Go, Falconbridge, now hast thou thy [[Desire\|desire]]:<br>A landless knight makes thee a landed squire.—<br>Come, madam, and come, [[Richard]]; we must [[Speed\|speed]]<br>For France; for France, for it is more than need. |
| BASTARD | Brother, adieu. Good fortune come to thee, 180<br>For thou wast got i’th’ way of honesty. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but the Bastard |
| BASTARD | A foot of [[Honour\|honour]] better than I was,<br>But many a many foot of land the worse.<br>[[Well]], now can I make any Joan a lady.<br>‘Good e’en, Sir [[Richard]]‘—’God-a-[[Mercy\|mercy]] fellow’;<br>And if his name be George I’ll call him Peter,<br>For new-made [[Honour\|honour]] doth forget men’s names;<br>’Tis too respective and too sociable<br>For your [[Conversion\|conversion]]. Now your traveller,<br>He and his toothpick at my worship’s mess; 190<br>And when my knightly stomach is sufficed,<br>Why then I suck my teeth and catechize<br>My picked man of countries. ‘My dear sir,’<br>Thus leaning on mine elbow I begin,<br>‘I shall beseech you—’.
